# Run log

Every cluster job, successful or not, in submission order. Checks run on
the login node or locally and design decisions are logged here too, as their
own rows. Every job also records itself in `$B2E_ROOT/runs.tsv` when it
starts (`cluster/common.sh`). Failures are explained in
[failures.md](failures.md) (F#). Times are US Eastern.

Results are copied from the job's own output files or from the source that
was read. Nothing here is estimated.

## 2026-10-24

| local | Decision: project scope and plan (docs/plan.md), approved by the user | — | done | — | Base model Qwen3.5-4B-Base; three domains (finance, law, medicine) fused by multi-teacher on-policy distillation; side track pre-trains a model from scratch with nanochat |
| local | Decision: one shared mid-training run (M0) instead of one per domain | — | done | — | Merging (TIES/DARE) and distillation baselines need the experts and the student to start from the same checkpoint. It also saves two mid-training runs. A finance-only M0 is kept as an ablation |
| local | Decision: no LLaMA-Factory | — | done | — | In sql-agent-rl it required transformers ≤5.6 (the env has 5.9) and produced non-finite gradients on half its steps (sql-agent-rl F#19, F#24). Mid-training and SFT use verl's SFT trainer, which ran cleanly there |
| local | Read Qwen3.5-4B-Base `config.json` and file list on the Hub | — | done | — | `Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration` (vision tower, depth 24, plus 1 MTP layer). Text: 32 layers in the pattern 3 × Gated DeltaNet then 1 × gated full attention; hidden 2560; vocab 248,320; tied embeddings; `mamba_ssm_dtype` float32; checkpoint 9.3 GB (2 shards) |
| login node | Package list of sql-agent-rl's packed env (`sar-verl0.9.1-vllm0.26.0-cu129-r1.freeze.txt`) | — | done | — | torch 2.11.0+cu129, vLLM 0.26.0, verl 0.9.1, transformers 5.9.0, TRL 0.27.0, lm-eval 0.4.13. **Not installed: flash-linear-attention, causal-conv1d, mergekit, datatrove, MLflow.** No Qwen3.5 weights downloaded |
| local | Read transformers 5.9.0 `models/qwen3_5/modeling_qwen3_5.py` (Gated DeltaNet forward, lines 437-560) | — | done | — | `Qwen3_5ForCausalLM` exists and skips `mtp.*` and `model.visual.*` on load, so a text-only fine-tune saved through it has no MTP head. **Packed sequences:** the delta-rule kernel resets its state at boundaries only through `cu_seqlens`, and only the flash-linear-attention kernel takes it. The torch fallback `torch_chunk_gated_delta_rule(..., **kwargs)` silently drops it. The short causal conv stops at boundaries only with the `causal_conv1d` kernel (`seq_idx`); otherwise it is a plain `conv1d` over the whole packed row, and the last 3 tokens of one document leak into the next. Both packages are therefore required before any packed (remove-padding) training (corrected below for training under verl) |
| local | Read verl's `examples/grpo_trainer/run_qwen3_5_2b_openr1_fsdp.sh` | — | done | — | verl's own Qwen3.5 recipe runs with `use_remove_padding=False`, micro-batch 1, FSDP2 with param and optimizer offload. It was tested with vllm 0.18.0 and a transformers main commit |
| local | Versions on PyPI | — | done | — | flash-linear-attention 0.6.0 was released 2026-10-23, the day before this env. The env pins 0.5.2 (2026-07-27). causal-conv1d 1.7.0 has no wheel for torch 2.11, so it is built on a CPU node (`cluster/build_ext.sbatch`) |

| login node | Base vs post-trained `tokenizer.json` diff | — | done | — | **The Base tokenizer does not register `<think>` (248068), `</think>` (248069), `<tool_response>` (248066), `</tool_response>` (248067)**; the post-trained one does. Base eos is `<|endoftext|>`, post-trained eos is `<|im_end|>`. With the Base tokenizer, SFT targets containing `<think>` would be split into ordinary pieces and the model would learn to spell the tag. Decision: Base weights + the post-trained model's tokenizer files (same vocab) |

The rows are trained; only the tokenizer registration is missing. No embedding repair needed (unlike sql-agent-rl F#29). To confirm behaviourally: p(`<think>`) at the answer start after SFT |
| local | Read verl 0.9.1 `verl/models/transformers/qwen3_5.py` and `workers/engine/fsdp/transformer_impl.py` | — | done | — | **Corrects the transformers row above for training under verl:** verl replaces the Gated DeltaNet forward. Without causal-conv1d it splits the packed row at `cu_seqlens` and runs the conv one segment at a time (`_packed_causal_conv1d_fallback`), which is correct, only slower. The delta rule still needs fla: verl passes `cu_seqlens` whenever the kernel *accepts* it (`_call_accepts_kwarg`), and the torch fallback's `**kwargs` counts as accepting it, so the boundary is dropped silently. verl passes `cu_seqlens` only to models whose patched `forward` takes it, i.e. the `Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration` class, not the text-only class. To be verified on a GPU by `tools/pack_check.py` |

| 11354648 | M0 head-start leg on gpu-short (2 h, `trainer.save_freq=80`), at the user's request to start M0 sooner; leg 1 (11353312) re-chained `afterany` on it, so two legs never write the checkpoint directory at once | gpu-short | submitted | — | Why it is safe to stop at a time limit: verl keeps the old checkpoint until the new one is fully written (with `max_ckpt_to_keep=1`, `ensure_checkpoint_capacity` removes nothing before a save) and updates `latest_checkpointed_iteration.txt` atomically after it, so resume never points at a half-written step. Shorter limits on the gpu partition did not move the estimated start (`sbatch --test-only`: 00:28 for 8 h, 4 h and 2 h); gpu-short estimated 23:38. | 11377320 | (result) M0, finance, no thinking, ×2 greedy, 4,096 tokens (same command as Base-chat 11347420) | gpu / d4053 (H200) | completed | 12m42s | **FinQA 0.612 (Base-chat 0.707), TAT-QA 0.673 (0.770).** Not a loss of finance skill in the plain sense: **M0 opens its own `<think>` block in every answer (1,147 / 1,147 and 738 / 738; Base-chat 0)** although the no-thinking template has already closed an empty one. FinQA answers grow from 397 / 385 to 1,807 / 1,872 tokens on average (run 0 / run 1; first written as "397 to 1,807", run 0 only) and 245-268 of 1,147 hit the 4,096 limit (Base-chat 19-28; TAT-QA 143 of 738, Base-chat 9-11); those score 0. (Corrected after a check: most of the cut answers are still deliberating, not repeating; only 24 of 245 repeat a 60-character passage 6+ times in their last 3,000 characters. I first wrote "usually in a loop" from one example.) On the answers that finish, M0 gets 0.788 FinQA / 0.835 TAT-QA (Base-chat 0.722 / 0.777), but the answers that finish are the easier questions, so that is not a like-for-like gain. Cause: the long-CoT share of the M0 mix (15%, OpenThoughts3 rendered with the chat template, `m0_mix.render_chat`) has only thinking-mode conversations, so mid-training taught "an assistant turn starts with reasoning" and the no-thinking switch was lost. SFT has to bring it back (the SFT mixes pair thinking and non-thinking answers); `run_eval` now reports `reopened_think`, the share of answers that open a `<think>` block, to measure the switch for every model from here on. The fair finance comparison for M0 is thinking mode (11377321) |
| 11377321 | (result) M0, finance, thinking, greedy, 8,192 tokens (same command as Base-chat 11349022) | gpu / d4053 (H200) | completed | 8m57s | **FinQA 0.622 (Base-chat 0.689), TAT-QA 0.682 (0.673).** No reopened `<think>` (the template opens it). The reasoning got shorter in the middle and longer in the tail: median 1,220 / 1,131 tokens (Base-chat 1,922 / 1,585), but **235 / 122 answers cut at 8,192 (Base-chat 110 / 32)**. The answers that finish are more often right than Base-chat's (FinQA 0.782 vs 0.761, TAT-QA 0.817 vs 0.701; again a selected subset). Of the cut answers only 16 / 3 repeat a 60-character passage 6+ times near the end; the rest keep deliberating. So M0's finance reasoning is better when it ends and ends less often; a likely contributor is the long OpenThoughts3 traces in the mix (not measured yet). For SFT this means teaching when to stop: the finance SFT answers are the teacher's shortest verified ones |

| — | (laptop) Question raised by 11377319: do Base-chat and M0 stop on the same tokens? | — | open | — | vLLM ends a generation at the tokenizer's eos plus the eos ids of the model's generation config, and with no `generation_config.json` it derives one from `config.json`. Base-chat's `config.json` (from the Base) has `eos_token_id` 248044 = `<|endoftext|>` only inside `text_config`; M0's, re-saved by verl, has 248046 = `<|im_end|>` at the top level. The pre-training part of the M0 mix ends every document with `<|endoftext|>`. If vLLM stops on `<|endoftext|>` for Base-chat but not for M0, M0's answers would run on after a proper end and the comparison would be unfair. Not known yet: `tools/stop_tokens.py` (new) prints the stop ids vLLM uses and counts answers that write an end token and continue (`skip_special_tokens=False`) |
| 11378220 | `tools/stop_tokens.py` on M0 and Base-chat, first 200 IFEval prompts, no thinking, greedy | gpu-short (A100 80 GB) | submitted | — | — |
| 11377318 | (result) M0, general + law/medicine, no thinking (same command as Base-chat 11352017) | gpu-short / d1029 (A100 80 GB) | completed | 58m45s (Base-chat 20m27s: longer answers) | M0 vs Base-chat accuracy: **GSM8K 0.901 vs 0.923, MMLU-Pro 0.571 vs 0.657, C-Eval 0.696 vs 0.770, CMMLU 0.724 vs 0.765, MedQA 0.702 vs 0.735, MedMCQA 0.614 vs 0.657, CMExam 0.703 vs 0.753, CAIL 0.335 vs 0.482.** Lower on every set, with the same pattern as finance: answers opening their own `<think>` (GSM8K 98%, CMExam 91%, C-Eval / CMMLU 75%, CAIL 65%, MMLU-Pro 59%, MedMCQA 15%, MedQA 10%), mean length 2-11× longer, and far more cut at 4,096 (CMExam 53 → 961 of 6,810, CAIL 4 → 298, MMLU-Pro 120 → 285). On the answers that finish M0 scores at or above Base-chat's overall number (GSM8K 0.971, C-Eval 0.819, CMExam 0.818; selected subsets again). Read together with the stop-token check (11378220) before any conclusion on forgetting |
| 11378220 | (result) `tools/stop_tokens.py`, M0 and Base-chat, 200 IFEval prompts, no thinking, special tokens kept | gpu-short / A100 80 GB | completed | — | **The two models do stop on different ids, but it changes nothing.** vLLM's derived generation config has `eos_token_id` 248044 (`<|endoftext|>`) for Base-chat and 248046 (`<|im_end|>`) for M0; both also stop on the tokenizer's eos, `<|im_end|>`. Every stopped answer of both models ended on 248046 (Base-chat 180 of 200, M0 141); neither wrote `<|endoftext|>` anywhere (for M0 it would have shown in the text, since it is not a stop id there). M0's 59 cut answers (Base-chat 20) are answers that do not end, not an end token that was ignored. The question from 11377319 is closed: the M0 drop on IF and general is the model's behaviour (reopened thinking, answers that run on), and the comparison with Base-chat is fair |

Against S-fin's train loss (11397405): end of epoch 1 0.319, end of epoch 2 0.197. Epoch 1's train loss is taken on rows before the model trains on them, so it stands for held-out loss; the final model's val loss, 0.322, is at that level: **epoch 2 lowered the loss on rows already seen (0.32 → 0.20), not on held-out rows.** The two averages weight tokens differently (per batch vs over the file), so a difference of ±0.01 means nothing; no sign of damage either, and what epoch 2 did to answers is for the evals. **The other three files are not clean for S-fin (F#47)**: each mix draws its own val split, so rows of the shared sources (IF, SmolTalk2) in mix_law / mix_med / mix_mix val can be in mix_fin's train, and so can mix_mix's finance rows. It shows: S-fin's if_synth loss is 0.11-0.16 on the other mixes' val rows against 0.38 on its own, mix_mix's finance rows 0.101 against 0.147. Rows S-fin cannot have seen: the law rows of mix_law val 0.686 (M0 1.064) and the medicine rows of mix_med val 0.613 (M0 0.773), lower although S-fin trained on neither; how much of that is the answer format shared by all three domains (same teacher) and how much law or medicine, this number cannot tell. A rerun scoring only unseen rows follows. |

| local | `cluster/rl.sbatch`: `NAME` (default `RUN`) names the checkpoint dir, the log and the W&B run, and is passed as `trainer.experiment_name` (123669c) | — | done | — | Before, the checkpoint dir was `$CKPT/$RUN`, the config name, so a short smoke of `grpo_fin` would have saved into `ckpt/grpo_fin` and the real run (`resume_mode: auto`) would have resumed from the smoke's step-3 checkpoint. With `NAME=grpo_fin_smoke` the smoke has its own dir. For the real runs nothing changes (`NAME` = `RUN` = each config's `experiment_name`). Also noted for P6: the RL val files are FinQA dev and TAT-QA dev, and TAT-QA dev is also the reported TAT-QA eval, so checkpoints are not picked by val score; each run is reported at its last step. |

The val split's makeup is close to the mix's (medicine 55% of the val target tokens vs 51% of the mix's tokens, if_synth 27% vs 30%), so it does not explain the gap; it made me look again at the comparison itself, which does not hold (F#49). Unseen rows of the other domains, M0 → S-med: finance (190) 0.706 → 0.274, non-thinking 0.352 → 0.285; law (68) 1.063 → 0.661, non-thinking 1.144 → 1.121. Same as S-law: another domain's SFT lowers the loss of finance's short answers, not of law's or medicine's. |

Val (128 of FinQA dev + TAT-QA dev, greedy, 35 s): FinQA 0.870 → 0.884, TAT-QA 0.949 → 0.898 after 3 steps (n = 69 / 59, noise). Train/inference mismatch small: rollout-vs-actor KL 1.5-1.7e-4, mean prob diff 0.0019. lr 1e-6 every step (W&B's summary prints 0.0, rounding). **Three findings** from the metrics and the rollout dumps (`ckpt/grpo_fin_smoke/rollouts/{1,2,3}.jsonl`, 1,024 each): (1) `actor/pg_clipfrac` 0 and `actor/ppo_kl` 0 at every step: `ppo_mini_batch_size` = `train_batch_size`, one update per step, so every ratio is 1 and the clips never act (F#54). (2) 148 of 3,072 answers never closed `</think>` (cut at the limit) and **57 of them scored 1**, from a `\boxed{}` written inside the reasoning (F#53). (3) **Half the groups are all-correct** (62 / 69 / 64 of 128 have 8/8; 6 / 1 / 3 have 0/8), so ~52% of the samples carry zero advantage, although the pool kept only prompts with 1-7 of 8 correct: selecting on 8 samples picks up many prompts whose true pass rate is near 1 and that got one wrong by chance, and a fresh 8 is then often 8/8 (regression to the mean). Not changed: it is the same for every arm, and it is what online dynamic sampling would fix (DAPO arm, later). Two answers had more than one `\boxed{}` after `</think>` ("\boxed{0.8225} (or \boxed{82.25\%})", scored on the last; "\boxed{3.37\%} \boxed{3.00\%}", 0). The end of the log has tracebacks from teardown (a DataLoader worker killed, W&B's offline service connection reset) after `Final validation metrics` and the save; the job exited 0. Checkpoint `global_step_3` 51 GB, to delete. |

| local | Fixes from the smoke (0f677cc): **(F#53)** `compute_score(..., thinking=False)`: an answer without `</think>` scores 0 when the prompt opened the block (`thinking=True`), and one that opens `<think>` itself must close it in either mode; verl gets `thinking` from `reward.custom_reward_function.reward_kwargs` (`${data.apply_chat_template_kwargs.enable_thinking}`, verl 0.9.1 binds these with `get_custom_reward_fn`), `run_eval` from `--thinking`, `synth` always thinking, `rescore_eval.py` a required `--thinking`. **(F#54)** `ppo_mini_batch_size` 32 in `base.yaml` (4 policy updates per step of 128 prompts, the GSPO paper's split) and 256 for PPO's actor and critic (1,024 prompts). **`LEG_STEPS`** in `rl.sbatch`: a leg sets `trainer.total_training_steps` to the steps done + LEG_STEPS (at most the config's total), so it ends at a checkpoint instead of being killed with the work since the last save lost (~5 steps ≈ 1 h per 8-h leg at save_freq 10). Safe because the lr schedule is constant (smoke: `lr_scheduler_type: constant`, no warm-up) and the V1 trainer's loop (`trainer_base.fit`) stops at `total_training_steps` with a save and a val; a leg that starts at the final step does only the val and exits. `check_rl_data.py` now scores with the function verl loads (reward_kwargs bound), checks a cut-off answer scores 0, and prints updates per step. `make lint test` 116 passed, mypy clean, CI success. | — | done | — | Kept as it was: lr 1e-6, 150 steps, n = 8, 4,096 tokens. Four updates per step make the 2nd-4th updates slightly off-policy, which is what clip ranges are for; DAPO's paper splits each step into 16, GSPO's into 4. |

| — | Decision: weight interpolation (WiSE-FT) of S-fin towards Base-chat, `tools/interpolate.py` (new): base + α (S-fin − base) per tensor in fp32, stored in bf16 with S-fin's config/tokenizer; α = 0.5 and 0.75 first | — | decided | — | S-fin's general scores are 1-5 points below Base-chat and the S-fin-bc ablation put most of that on the SFT (11418651). Interpolating back is the cheapest way to trade some finance gain for general ability: no training, one eval pair per α on gpu-short (a separate QoS, so P6's gpu slots are untouched). Base-chat holds the Base weights (`make_chat_model.sh`), so this is the classic fine-tune-vs-pretrained interpolation; for S-fin the path also undoes M0. Header check first (`jobs/st_names.py`): same 723 tensor names and shapes, Base-chat additionally has the 15 `mtp.*` tensors (S-fin's config: `mtp_num_hidden_layers: 0`, left out) and stores 48 Gated DeltaNet tensors (`A_log`, `linear_attn.norm`) in fp32 where S-fin has bf16. mergekit is kept for P8's TIES/DARE; it too drops the MTP head (11346557). |

Rescoring every rollout with `compute_score` gives the logged score (0 differences; for DAPO only where its overlong penalty applies). **Wrong answers** (GRPO 5, DAPO 4, GSPO 7, PPO 7 of 50) are mostly the gold's conventions or label noise: the sign of losses, deficits and reversed periods ("from december 2012 to december 2011": 8 of 8 answer −394, gold 394), change vs rate of change, points vs relative change, an "in thousands" question whose gold is in millions, a question on a year the excerpt lacks where "Not available" scores 0; a few misreadings; one answer per arm ran to 4,096 (DAPO: −1 from its penalty). Sign misses stay at 37-46 per step while the other misses fall (GRPO 124 → 45): they sit in a few prompts (8 at step 60; 2 with no right sample, which give no gradient). Abstentions (a box with no number) 0-1 % per step, and 0 at steps 40, 50 and 60 in GRPO and DAPO: the direction the reward pushes (an abstention scores 0), too rare to call. **Learning signal**: prompts whose 8 samples did not all score the same (the only ones with a nonzero GRPO advantage) fall from 60 % (GRPO step 1), 59 % (GSPO), 80 % (DAPO, whose penalty splits groups) to 12-30 % at GRPO steps 40-60, with 85 % of step 60's prompts all right: the pool has 2,308 prompts, one pass per 18 steps, so step 60 is the 4th pass (F#58). |

| — | AutoResearch keep rule `--confirm` (8580136, CI success): the best is the mean of all its runs; a candidate whose first run beats it by `--screen-sd` (1) pooled standard deviations (`screen`) is trained `--confirm` (2) more times alternating with the best (`confirm` / `recheck`), and is `kept` only if its mean beats the mean of those best runs by two standard errors of the difference and by at least `--min-gain`; else `rejected`. The standard deviation is pooled over every commit trained more than once in the stretch, so the margin follows the node's noise (11360201's 0.00025 against 11378056's 0.0039). `runs.jsonl` keeps every run's summary (steps, MFU), which the noise analysis lacked. `program.md` tells the researcher the new statuses and that small effects cannot be seen | local | done | — | Tested end to end with a stand-in train.py (a constant that lowers val_bpb by 0.01, plus fixed noise): baseline ×3, the real change kept after screen → recheck → confirm → recheck → confirm, a null change rejected, the file and HEAD at the kept change. A candidate with no effect passes the screen about one time in five (P(Z < −1 / √(1 + 1/n)) for a best measured n times: 19 % at n = 3, 16 % for large n), so most rounds still cost one run and a confirmed round five. |

At 8,192, 963 of the 7,200 samples (13.4 %; MedQA 680, 28.3 %) finished in 4,097-8,192 tokens and are right 65.5 % of the time (MedQA 70.9 %), against 81.3 % for those that finished within 4,096; a 4,096 cap scores them all 0. Scoring every answer of the 8,192 run longer than 4,096 tokens as 0 gives 0.668, the 4,096 run 0.670; mean score per prompt 8,192 − 4,096 = +0.086 (se 0.006), MedQA +0.196 (se 0.013). The runs share seed 0 and vLLM seeds each sample: 183 of the 5,481 sample pairs that finished within 4,096 in both runs have the same length and answer at the same position (3.3 %, against 0.04 % at different positions), so those are likely the same text; the others diverged. Example, MedQA 4662 (gold B): at 4,096, six answers B in 1,629-3,802 tokens and two cut, so GRPO gives the six +0.54 and the two −1.62 (mean 0.75, std 0.463) and it is kept; at 8,192 all eight answer B, in 1,015-6,005 tokens. |

| — | **Decision, P7 response caps: medicine 8,192, law 4,096** (finance stays at P6's 4,096) | — | decided | — | The cap at which the starting policy's cut share is about P6's 4.4 %: medicine 4.4 % at 8,192 (18.0 % at 4,096), law 6.0 % at 4,096. At 4,096 MedQA's signal would be mostly finishing in time (92 % of its kept prompts' wrong samples cut) and would score 0 the 28 % of its answers that took 4,097-8,192 tokens, 71 % of them right; the thinking eval allows 8,192 (finance's evals: 8,192). Law's kept prompts are there for their answers (7.1 % only because of cuts). Cost: medicine rollouts are longer (2,649 tokens a sample against 2,247 at 4,096). The RL configs follow with the P7 recipe (after PPO). |

Then S-law and S-med sample k = 8 at the RL settings and the domain's cap, on draws sized for ~6,400 kept at the pilots' kept shares (F#58, 150 steps): **law** term 3,000, charges all 7,827, articles all 7,688 (expected kept 2,320, 1,435, 2,716; 379 M tokens, ~9.7 H200 hours at the pilot's 10,903 tokens/s); **medicine** MedQA 4,400, MedMCQA 5,100, CMExam 5,600 (2,156, 2,159, 2,147; 310 M tokens, ~7.4 h at 11,562/s) | — | planned | — | Charges keeps 18 %, so 3.3 h of the law sampling buys its 1,435 prompts; leaving charges out would leave a third of CAIL untrained. Chunks of 500, so a job cut by its time limit loses one chunk at most (~23 min of MedQA at 8,192). |

TRL's default completion ends `<|im_end|>`, newline, `<|im_end|>` on all 3,770 (the template's turn end plus TRL's eos): two tokens the model never generates (it stops at the first `<|im_end|>`) inside every completion's log-probability. The rendered completions are what S-fin generated: completion minus generated tokens -0.02 on average (-3 to +5), equal for 97.6 %, so the pairs stay on-policy. Lengths: prompt max 3,051 (p99 1,972, mean 880), completion with eos max 4,096 (p99 4,039), together max 6,903; TRL's defaults (512, 1,024) would have cut nearly every pair, so the P5 configs need `max_prompt_length` >= 3,051 and `max_length` >= 6,903. |

| — | P5 training stack (local; CI success on each push): `b2e.train.pref_train` + `cluster/pref.sbatch` + `configs/po/{dpo,simpo,kto,orpo}_fin.yaml`. TRL 0.27: DPOTrainer; CPO (simpo loss, cpo_alpha 0), KTO and ORPO from `trl.experimental` (the `trl.trainer` names only forward there and warn; removed in 0.29). Policy loaded in fp32 and trained under bf16 autocast, so AdamW updates fp32 weights (a bf16 weight cannot hold a 5e-7-sized update: with 8 significant bits, round-to-nearest drops a change below half the spacing, ~0.2-0.4 % of the weight, and Adam's step at 5e-7 is ~1e-5 of it); the vision tower of `Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration` frozen but kept, so the export has S-fin's architecture (`check_arch`); DPO and KTO against S-fin in bf16, frozen. Before training every row the trainer built is checked against prompt + completion + eos (each trainer tokenizes with its own code: DPO `tokenize_row`, CPO/ORPO `build_tokenized_answer`, KTO `_tokenize`); `--padding-check` compares log-probs alone and right-padded in a batch (Qwen3.5's linear-attention state); peak GPU memory and runtime printed after training. | — | Two P5 fixes (4a55d99; 171 tests). (1) **KTO's KL rows** (F#76). Reading `trl/experimental/kto/kto_trainer.py` (0.27.0) for the dry check showed how TRL builds KTO's reference point: at load time it gives each row the completion of its neighbour, in batches of `per_device_train_batch_size` rows in dataset order (`_get_kl_dataset`, with no shuffle before it). `unpaired()` put each pair's chosen and rejected side by side, so with batches of 2 every KL row would have been the other answer to its own prompt. That is a matched pair, which the KL estimate must not use; TRL's own error for batch size 1 names the same failure. Now row 2k is pair k's chosen and row 2k+1 the rejected of pair k+s (pairs sorted by prompt, s the most pairs one prompt has). The two prompts differ, and every answer is used once. `check_kl_rows` checks every KL row the trainer built (another prompt's completion, the expected tokens, the label mask) and stops the run otherwise. (2) **`fix_trl_availability()`** (F#75): before any trainer import, TRL's copy of `_is_package_available` is wrapped so that it returns a bool again without `return_version`. It applies only when the helper returns a tuple. The env is not changed, because PPO, the pool samplings and the evals use it | local | done | — | Both were found before any GPU time was spent. Unit tests cover: the new row order on 3 prompts x 2 pairs and on 2 x 2 (each answer once, each row next to another prompt); refusal when one prompt has more than half the pairs; `kl_partner` against TRL's rotation, including a short last batch; `check_kl_rows` passing the new order and flagging all 12 rows of the old one; the wrapper returning bools and keeping the version tuple. |

| — | **Review of every stage against 2026 practice** (the user: 「看看每一步的技术是不是结合实际的、先进的」, 「还有 RSI」), read 14:0x-14:3x EST: DeepSeek-V4 (arXiv 2606.19348, abstract; its post-training via M. Labonne's reading), Kimi K3 (arXiv 2607.24653, the post-training section), MOPD (2606.30406), Open-MOPD (2608.19098), Generalized PPO (2610.13015), VeXact (2605.14220), Rubrics as Rewards, R-Quest (2610.04299), CoVerRL (2603.17775), DARC (2601.13761), SPICE (2510.24684), SkyPilot's scaled AutoResearch run, ShinkaEvolve (2509.19349) | — | done | — | **Already current**: the hybrid linear-attention base (K3 builds on Kimi Delta Attention, DeepSeek-V4 on hybrid compressed attention), Muon in the side track (DeepSeek-V4 pre-trains with it), domain experts by RL consolidated by multi-teacher on-policy distillation (K3: nine RL experts, three domains × three effort levels, then MOPD; DeepSeek-V4 reportedly drops mixed RL for distillation; the MOPD paper's baselines are mix-RL, cascade RL, off-policy finetune and parameter merge, as in our P8), the training-inference gap logged every step (law step 30: `rollout_probs_diff` mean 0.0032, max 0.34; VeXact shows such a gap alone can collapse a run), and P5's result (K3's pipeline has no separate DPO stage; ours puts offline PO ~2 points under DAPO). **Older than current practice**: the planned Bradley-Terry reward model (K3 scores non-verifiable tasks with a generative judge), an L1-style length reward for the thinking budget (K3: a per-prompt budget from the cold-start model, -1 over it), the sampled per-token reverse KL in P8 (DeepSeek-V4 reportedly uses a full-vocabulary logit KL), no token balancing across domains in P8 (Open-MOPD: plain MOPD recovers 35.6 % of the headroom, balanced 83.4 %), and the RSI proposer inventing figures with a majority vote as the check (R-Quest, CoVerRL, DARC). |
| — | **Decision (user, 14:39 EST)** on the review: every proposed upgrade adopted | — | decided | — | (1) P8: the KL estimator (sampled reverse KL vs full-vocabulary or top-k logit KL) and Open-MOPD's token-share balancing with gap-aware allocation as core comparisons; (2) P7.5 redesign: proposals grounded in a real filing's text and table, a validity gate before the pass-rate filter (Qwen3.6-35B-A3B judges answerable and unambiguous and answers independently; kept only if both hold and it matches the program), semantic dedup by a frozen model, 20 kept problems read by hand per round; (3) P5: the Bradley-Terry reward model dropped for a rubric judge in K3's protocol (a rubric per prompt, pairwise tournament within the group, over a verbosity limit loses), measured against HealthBench physician grades before it rewards medicine's open prompts; (4) effort control: R-fin-low from R-fin with K3's per-prompt budget (S-fin's pool-sampling length × a multiplier annealed down, -1 over budget), routed in P8 by an effort flag; (5) P2.5: two trials per gpu-short job, one per GPU, and a candidate pool with parent sampling and a novelty check, the `--confirm` keep rule unchanged. **Not added** (recommended against in the review): another RL algorithm arm (2026's variants are small changes of the four P6 compared), agentic multi-turn RL (sql-agent-rl covers it), MXFP4 quantization-aware training (needs Blackwell; H200 has no native FP4, P9 stays FP8). Order: P7's legs keep running; CPU work first on what verl 0.9.1 supports for P8 and on the P7.5 redesign; then the P7.5 pilot, R-fin-low, the rubric judge, the P2.5 stretches, P8. docs/plan.md updated. |
| local | What verl 0.9.1 gives P8 (read from the v0.9.1 tag, 1876b06: `verl/workers/config/distillation.py`, `verl/trainer/distillation/`, `verl/experimental/teacher_loop/`) | — | done | — | **Routing**: several teachers under `distillation.teacher_models`, each with a `key`; samples go to the teacher whose key equals their `distillation.teacher_key` field (default `data_source`). **Losses**: estimators on the sampled token (`kl`/`k1`, `k2`/`mse`, `k3`/`low_var_kl`, `abs`), in policy-gradient form (Thinking Machines; `k1` recommended) or backpropagated directly (`k3`); and `forward_kl_topk`, a forward KL over the teacher's top-k log-probs (dataclass default k = 128, yaml 32; a chunked path for long contexts), which the code warns is meant for the supervised form. The teacher log-probs come from vLLM `prompt_logprobs`, so **no full-vocabulary KL**: P8's comparison is sampled reverse KL against top-k forward KL, with k chosen from the teacher mass verl logs (`teacher_mass`). It also logs teacher/student top-k overlap (after arXiv 2604.13016). **GPUs**: each teacher's replicas take their own GPUs from a dedicated pool (`split_resource_pool` gives disjoint bundles; no external endpoint), so three teachers and the student need 4 GPUs in one Ray cluster, which no single job has under the QoS (gpu 1 GPU per job, gpu-short 2). P0's option (c), the three 4B teachers sharing one GPU, needs a patch (fractional-GPU bundles in the teacher pool, or a client for external vLLM servers), to be smoke-tested on gpu-short. **Token balancing** is not in verl: a sampler that sets each domain's prompt share (data level) or per-domain loss weights; Open-MOPD's method is read before choosing. |

| local | Open-MOPD's method read (arXiv 2608.19098, HTML, first 100 K of 119 K characters) for P8's balancing arm | — | done | — | (1) **Token-share balancing**: domain d's token share s_d = n_d·L_d / Σ n_j·L_j (prompts × mean response length); loss weight w_d = g*_d / s_d with target shares g* equal across domains; token-mean loss, sampling unchanged (their IF weight came out ~32.7 against ~0.7 for math and code). (2) **Gap-aware allocation**: m_d = a running mean of the per-token reward magnitude |r| in domain d (an EMA, decay not given), updated every step; w̃_d = w_d·clamp((m_d / mean m)^α, 0.05, 20), renormalised so the total loss scale stays; α = 1 (α = −0.5 collapsed at step 74). (3) **Student reward refresh**: with K minibatches per rollout the dense reward goes stale (rollout-to-current KL 0.059 at K = 4, 0.216 at K = 32); it is recomputed before each inner update from the current student's log-probs on the cached teacher top-k, at no extra forward pass. Their reward is a top-k (k = 16, the student's top-k) sum of δ_t(v)·π̃(v). Ablation on SmolLM3-3B: 28.05 → +1.17 (share) → +0.72 (gap) → +0.81 (refresh at K = 4), 31.24 in all. For us: verl aggregates the loss over the batch without per-domain weights, so (1) and (2) need per-sample weights (or advantages scaled per domain in the policy-gradient form); our runs use 4 minibatches per step (K = 4), where (3) matters. To be built and smoke-tested with P8's teacher-sharing patch. |

| local | P8 Open-MOPD loss weights (92412cc, CI success; 8 new tests, 238 in all): `b2e.distill.balance`. Token-share balancing (Open-MOPD Eqs. 6, 8): s_d = domain d's share of the batch's response tokens, w_d = (1/D) / s_d, so every routing key gets the same effective share; gap-aware (Eq. 9): w_d × clamp((m_d / mean m)^1, 0.05, 20), renormalised to Σ w_d s_d = 1, m_d an EMA (decay 0.9, ours; the paper gives none) of domain d's mean per-token \|loss\| at rollout time (verl's estimator between old_log_probs and the teacher's log-probs, sliced as verl's loss slices them). Wired in at import like the teacher sharing (the hook moves to `b2e.distill.hook`): RayPPOTrainer._update_actor puts one weight per sample into the batch; every registered distillation loss multiplies its per-token losses by it, after clamping them at 10 itself, because verl's own clamp (loss_max_clamp) comes after the loss function and would cut the weighted values, so a weighted run sets it to null and the wrapper refuses to run otherwise. The trainer stops if the actor's metrics lack `b2e/weighted` (the weights never reached the loss). Gap weights need the sampled token's teacher log-prob, so a top-k loss supports share only. Not done: the paper's per-mini-batch reward refresh. `MOPD_BALANCE=share\|gap` in `cluster/mopd.sbatch` | local | done | — | Unit-tested math only (no torch locally); the GPU check is 11601495. |
